1	\vspace{-8pt}
2	\section{Conclusion}
3	\label{sec:conclusion}
4	
5	\looseness=-1
6	Across six METABENCH benchmarks, 17.9--31.3\% of items exhibit temporal measurement instability between 2023 and 2024 model cohorts (${\approx}$27--29\% for MMLU after non-independence corrections; conservative bound: 13.5\%). Web-overlap labels are near-independent of DIF flags (Jaccard $= 0.206$); rankings are preserved ($\rho \approx 0.997$). We recommend MH-DIF audits at $\geq$500 models per cohort.
7

[Director 指令] [Patch] 新实验数据补丁 — enrichment CI + Jaccard permutation null

两个补充实验完成，数据需要 patch 进论文：

**1. Li web-overlap enrichment 95% CI（patch §4.3 + Table 2）**
- Enrichment = 0.978, bootstrap 95% CI [0.944, 1.017]（CI 包含 1.0）
- Fisher exact: OR=0.948, p=0.25
- φ = -0.012
- 注意：Table 2 当前 enrichment=0.964，新 bootstrap 计算=0.978，两者差异来自 bootstrap resampling。建议在正文 §4.3 加 CI，Table 保持 0.964 不变（table 是点估计，正文报 bootstrap CI）

§4.3 当前文本 "enrichment ratio is 0.964" 附近，加 bootstrap CI：
→ 建议改为类似 "The enrichment ratio is 0.964 (bootstrap 95% CI: [0.94, 1.02]; \Cref{tab:temporal_landscape})"

§4.3 当前 "$\phi = 0.012$, OR $= 1.05$ [0.96, 1.15], Jaccard $= 0.206$" 保持不变（已有 OR CI）。

**2. Jaccard permutation null（patch §4.3 + §5.1）**
- Observed Jaccard = 0.206
- Permutation null: mean=0.212, SD=0.0049, 95% CI [0.203, 0.222]
- p = 0.88 (one-sided, observed vs null)
- 结论：observed Jaccard 在 null distribution 之内，overlap 完全由 marginal rates 预测

§4.3 当前 "Jaccard $= 0.206$"：
→ 改为 "Jaccard $= 0.206$ (within permutation null $[0.203, 0.222]$; $p = 0.88$)"

§5.1 discussion 当前 "Web-overlap labels...are near-independent of DIF flags (Jaccard $= 0.206$; \Cref{sec:groundtruth})"：
→ 加 permutation context: "(Jaccard $= 0.206$, indistinguishable from permutation null; \Cref{sec:groundtruth})"

这两个数据点把 "near-independent" 从描述性判断升级为统计性结论。这次不用验证引用。

_source line 5908 · 2026-05-22T16:09:32.406952+00:00_

44	\subsection{Ground Truth Mismatch}
45	\label{sec:groundtruth}
46	
47	\looseness=-1
48	Direct comparison of DIF flags against the web-overlap labels of \citet{li2024contamination} on 9,229 annotated items confirms that the two methods capture orthogonal signals. The enrichment ratio is 0.964 (\Cref{tab:temporal_landscape}): DIF-C rates are 30.7\% among web-contaminated items and 31.8\% among clean items. Association tests confirm near-independence ($\phi = 0.012$, OR $= 1.05$ [0.96, 1.15], Jaccard $= 0.206$). This near-independence holds across contamination subtypes (input-only enrichment $= 0.986$, input-and-label $= 0.960$). The null result does not indicate detection failure: semi-synthetic injection produces a clear dose-response ($\Delta\mathrm{TPR} = {+}0.508$ at $p = 0.5$; \Cref{fig:dose_response}). The disconnect arises because web-overlap labels measure \emph{global exposure} (whether an item exists in training corpora), while DIF measures \emph{differential functioning} (whether an item's relationship to ability changes across cohorts). The Li et al.\ labels are the wrong ground truth for a differential method. 
49	\begin{figure}[t]
50	\centering
51	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{fig_2_dose_response.pdf}
52	\caption{Semi-synthetic dose-response for DIF detection on MMLU. $\Delta\mathrm{TPR}$ increases monotonically with exploitation rate $p$, confirming that MH-DIF is sensitive to differential item behavior when present. Baseline (no injection) produces $\Delta\mathrm{TPR} \approx 0$.}
53	\label{fig:dose_response}
54	\end{figure}
55	
56	\subsection{Structural Analysis}
57	\label{sec:structural}

_source line 5910 · 2026-05-22T16:09:38.992567+00:00_

11	\looseness=-1
12	Our experimental evidence constrains this ambiguity through four exclusion arguments. \emph{Ability confounds} explain at most 2.4~pp: 1:1 matching yields C\% $= 28.9\%$ vs.\ unmatched 31.3\% (Cohen's $d = -0.17$; \Cref{sec:temporal}). \emph{Domain-level improvement} explains ${<}0.1\%$ of item-level DIF variance (pseudo $R^2 = 0.0007$); DIF is predominantly item-specific, not a byproduct of aggregate progress. \emph{Item quality defects} are ruled out: expert-annotated errors~\citep{gema2024mmlu} show no enrichment among DIF-C items ($0.92\times$, $p = 0.37$). \emph{Web-overlap labels}~\citep{li2024contamination} are near-independent of DIF flags (Jaccard $= 0.206$; \Cref{sec:groundtruth}), reflecting a category error in ground truth rather than detection failure; MMLU-CF~\citep{zhao2025mmlucf} complements our approach via item reformulation.
13	
14	\looseness=-1
15	The difficulty--direction interaction ($\rho = -0.597$; \Cref{sec:temporal}) provides suggestive evidence for capability evolution: hardest-decile DIF-C items are 88.7\% focal-favoring (2024 better), easiest-decile 96.2\% reference-favoring. This pattern is more consistent with skill acquisition than contamination, which would target items regardless of difficulty; however, temporal DIF is necessary but not sufficient for construct-irrelevant change, and observational DIF alone cannot separate contamination from capability evolution~\citep{suk2026rethinking}. The high aggregate $\rho \approx 0.997$ does not undermine DIF's utility: aggregate stability masks subject-level instability (mean per-subject $\rho = 0.950$; 22/57 below 0.95; five below 0.90). DIF monitoring enables targeted replacement~\citep{zhao2025mmlucf,habba2026growingpains}.

10	
11	\looseness=-1
12	Our experimental evidence constrains this ambiguity through four exclusion arguments. \emph{Ability confounds} explain at most 2.4~pp: 1:1 matching yields C\% $= 28.9\%$ vs.\ unmatched 31.3\% (Cohen's $d = -0.17$; \Cref{sec:temporal}). \emph{Domain-level improvement} explains ${<}0.1\%$ of item-level DIF variance (pseudo $R^2 = 0.0007$); DIF is predominantly item-specific, not a byproduct of aggregate progress. \emph{Item quality defects} are ruled out: expert-annotated errors~\citep{gema2024mmlu} show no enrichment among DIF-C items ($0.92\times$, $p = 0.37$). \emph{Web-overlap labels}~\citep{li2024contamination} are near-independent of DIF flags (Jaccard $= 0.206$, indistinguishable from permutation null; \Cref{sec:groundtruth}), reflecting a category error in ground truth rather than detection failure; MMLU-CF~\citep{zhao2025mmlucf} complements our approach via item reformulation.
13

diff --git a/paper/sections/discussion.tex b/paper/sections/discussion.tex
index c19b933..ffbb6c1 100644
--- a/paper/sections/discussion.tex
+++ b/paper/sections/discussion.tex
@@ -9,7 +9,7 @@
 Temporal DIF detects \emph{that} items function differently across cohorts, not \emph{why}. Three non-exclusive mechanisms may drive the observed ${\approx}$27--29\% rate: (1)~differential contamination (asymmetric training data exposure); (2)~capability evolution (genuine skill acquisition); (3)~training methodology shifts (RLHF, post-training). The ETS baseline of 5--15\% C\%~\citep{clauser1998dif} is for concurrent demographic groups sharing the same test administration; the comparison to temporal LLM cohorts is illustrative, not normative.
 
 \looseness=-1
-Our experimental evidence constrains this ambiguity through four exclusion arguments. \emph{Ability confounds} explain at most 2.4~pp: 1:1 matching yields C\% $= 28.9\%$ vs.\ unmatched 31.3\% (Cohen's $d = -0.17$; \Cref{sec:temporal}). \emph{Domain-level improvement} explains ${<}0.1\%$ of item-level DIF variance (pseudo $R^2 = 0.0007$); DIF is predominantly item-specific, not a byproduct of aggregate progress. \emph{Item quality defects} are ruled out: expert-annotated errors~\citep{gema2024mmlu} show no enrichment among DIF-C items ($0.92\times$, $p = 0.37$). \emph{Web-overlap labels}~\citep{li2024contamination} are near-independent of DIF flags (Jaccard $= 0.206$; \Cref{sec:groundtruth}), reflecting a category error in ground truth rather than detection failure; MMLU-CF~\citep{zhao2025mmlucf} complements our approach via item reformulation.

1	\section{Discussion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	\enlargethispage*{14\baselineskip}
4	
5	\subsection{What Drives Temporal DIF?}
6	\label{sec:disc_temporal}
7	
8	\looseness=-1
9	Temporal DIF detects \emph{that} items function differently across cohorts, not \emph{why}. Three non-exclusive mechanisms may drive the observed ${\approx}$27--29\% rate: (1)~differential contamination (asymmetric training data exposure); (2)~capability evolution (genuine skill acquisition); (3)~training methodology shifts (RLHF, post-training). The ETS baseline of 5--15\% C\%~\citep{clauser1998dif} is for concurrent demographic groups sharing the same test administration; the comparison to temporal LLM cohorts is illustrative, not normative.
10	
11	\looseness=-1
12	Our experimental evidence constrains this ambiguity through four exclusion arguments. \emph{Ability confounds} explain at most 2.4~pp: 1:1 matching yields C\% $= 28.9\%$ vs.\ unmatched 31.3\% (Cohen's $d = -0.17$; \Cref{sec:temporal}). \emph{Domain-level improvement} explains ${<}0.1\%$ of item-level DIF variance (pseudo $R^2 = 0.0007$); DIF is predominantly item-specific. \emph{Item quality defects} are ruled out: expert-annotated errors~\citep{gema2024mmlu} show no enrichment among DIF-C items ($0.92\times$, $p = 0.37$). \emph{Web-overlap labels}~\citep{li2024contamination} are near-independent of DIF flags (Jaccard $= 0.206$, indistinguishable from permutation null; \Cref{sec:groundtruth}), a category error in ground truth; MMLU-CF~\citep{zhao2025mmlucf} complements our approach via item reformulation.
13	
14	\looseness=-1
15	The difficulty--direction interaction ($\rho = -0.597$; \Cref{sec:temporal}) provides suggestive evidence for capability evolution: hardest-decile DIF-C items are 88.7\% focal-favoring (2024 better), easiest-decile 96.2\% reference-favoring. This pattern is more consistent with skill acquisition than contamination, which would target items regardless of difficulty; however, temporal DIF is necessary but

_source line 5996 · 2026-05-22T16:31:28.366664+00:00_

[Director 指令] [Patch] R18 审稿回应——非数据依赖的写作修复（批量）

R18 评分 3.5。以下是不需要等新实验数据的写作修复，请一并处理：

---

**1. W-C2: 因果排除论证 target 标注（§5.1 discussion.tex）**

当前 §5.1 四个排除论证的 target hypothesis 不够显式。请在每个排除论证后加明确的排除 target：

- "Ability confounds explain at most 2.4 pp" → 保持不变（target 已清楚: ability mismatch）
- "Domain-level improvement explains <0.1%" → 保持不变（target 已清楚: aggregate progress）
- "Item quality defects are ruled out: expert-annotated errors..." → 这个排除的是 **item defects as confound**，当前措辞已经说了 "Item quality defects are ruled out"，保持
- "Web-overlap labels are near-independent..." → 这个排除的是 **memorization as confound**。当前措辞 "a category error in ground truth" 不够明确。改为类似 "ruling out memorization as the primary driver"

关键：Reviewer 认为 Gema 和 Li 排除论证重复。它们不重复——Gema 排除 item defects，Li 排除 memorization。确保叙事让这个区别显而易见。

---

**2. W-C2: 审计协议 DIF-cause triage（§5.2 discussion.tex）**

当前 §5.2 的 Monitor/Flag/Act 不区分 DIF 成因。加 1 句 guidance：
→ "When difficulty--direction signals indicate capability evolution (hard items favor focal cohort), equating should preserve these gains; when DIF is difficulty-invariant, contamination is the likelier driver and item replacement should be prioritized."

---

**3. W-S1: Contribution 措辞校准（§1 introduction.tex page 2）**

改写三条 contributions，明确区分 empirical 和 methodological：
- C1（ground truth meta-critique）→ 标为 empirical finding
- C2（cross-subject matching + DEFF framework）→ 标为 methodological adaptation for LLM setting
- C3（temporal instability at scale）→ 标为 empirical finding

不需要大改，只需在 contribution 描述中加 "empirically demonstrate" / "adapt and validate" 等动词区分。

---

**4. W-S2: Ackerman 1992 Multidimensionality caveat（§5.1 或 Limitations）**

加 1-2 句：
→ "Multidimensional DIF~\citep{ackerman1992multidimensional} may arise when MMLU's 57 subjects span distinct latent dimensions; cross-subject matching mitigates between-domain multidimensionality but not within-domain violations."

如果 ackerman1992multidimensional 不在 bib 里，请添加。

---

**5. W-S4: 审计阈值措辞（§5.2）**

将 15%/25% 的措辞改为 "recommended starting points"：
→ "with three action levels as recommended starting points (calibrate per benchmark using ρ-degradation curves; \Cref{app:fig_rho_degradation})"

---

**6. W-S5: Growing Pains 区分扩展（§2 related_work.tex）**

当前 "prescriptive framework for maintaining comparability via fixed-parameter calibration" 扩展至 2-3 句：
→ 加 "While Growing Pains prescribes how to equate scores after instability is detected, our work diagnoses which items exhibit instability and quantifies its magnitude—the diagnostic step that must precede equating. Their anchor-item selection can directly consume our stable-item set as pre-screened candidates."

---

**7. Minor fixes:**

W-m1（§3.4 or §4.5）: 加 "upper-bound estimate under uniform binary flipping" 显式声明到 semi-synthetic framework 描述中

W-m2（§4.4）: 加 1 句解释 uniform vs non-uniform DIF 关系：
→ "The 47% union comprises 27--29% uniform DIF (detected by MH) and an additional 17.7% non-uniform DIF (interaction-only); the two categories are mutually exclusive by construction."

W-m4: "conservative lower bound" → "lower bound under weighted-mean DEFF assumptions" （在 §4.2 和 Limitations 中）

W-m6: 加 IRT caveat 到 §4.4 或 Limitations：
→ "IRT parameters are used only for auxiliary power analysis; all core DIF results are nonparametric."

W-m7: §3.4 首次引入 ΔFPR/ΔTPR 时加 formal definition

W-m8: Limitations 压缩——移除结果性数值（ΔTPR 等）到 appendix，保留 3 个核心点（binary flipping upper bound, non-independence, matching boundary）

---

**注意页面预算**：8 页限制。大部分修改是 word-level 替换。W-m8 Limitations 压缩会释放空间。如果空间紧张，W-S5 和 W-S2 可以移到 Limitations 段落。

这次不用验证引用（除 Ackerman 1992 需要确认/添加 bib entry）。

1	\section{Discussion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	\enlargethispage*{14\baselineskip}
4	
5	\subsection{What Drives Temporal DIF?}
6	\label{sec:disc_temporal}
7	
8	\looseness=-1
9	Temporal DIF detects \emph{that} items function differently across cohorts, not \emph{why}. Three non-exclusive mechanisms may drive the observed ${\approx}$27--29\% rate: (1)~differential contamination (asymmetric training data exposure); (2)~capability evolution (genuine skill acquisition); (3)~training methodology shifts (RLHF, post-training). The ETS baseline of 5--15\% C\%~\citep{clauser1998dif} is for concurrent demographic groups sharing the same test administration; the comparison to temporal LLM cohorts is illustrative, not normative.
10	
11	\looseness=-1
12	Our experimental evidence constrains this ambiguity through four exclusion arguments. \emph{Ability confounds} explain at most 2.4~pp: 1:1 matching yields C\% $= 28.9\%$ vs.\ unmatched 31.3\% (Cohen's $d = -0.17$; \Cref{sec:temporal}). \emph{Domain-level improvement} explains ${<}0.1\%$ of item-level DIF variance (pseudo $R^2 = 0.0007$); DIF is predominantly item-specific. \emph{Item quality defects} are ruled out: expert-annotated errors~\citep{gema2024mmlu} show no enrichment among DIF-C items (enrichment $= 1.10$, 95\% CI $[0.92, 1.27]$, $p = 0.23$). \emph{Web-overlap labels}~\citep{li2024contamination} are near-independent of DIF flags (Jaccard $= 0.206$, indistinguishable from permutation null; \Cref{sec:groundtruth}), a category error in ground truth; MMLU-CF~\citep{zhao2025mmlucf} complements our approach via item reformulation.
13	
14	\looseness=-1

1	\section{Introduction}
2	\label{sec:introduction}
3	
4	When we say ``Model B outperforms Model A on MMLU,'' we implicitly assume that the benchmark measures the same construct in the same way for both models. This assumption of temporal comparability underpins every cross-generation comparison published on leaderboards and in model reports, yet it is rarely tested. We use \emph{measurement invariance} to denote the property that test items function identically across groups~\citep{meredith1993invariance}, \emph{differential item functioning} (DIF) as the item-level statistical test for violations, and \emph{temporal measurement instability} as the empirical phenomenon when DIF is detected across time-defined cohorts---a descriptive term denoting systematic changes in measurement properties, without implying degradation or attributing cause. Applying Mantel-Haenszel DIF analysis~\citep{holland1988mh} with ETS severity classification to the METABENCH response matrix~\citep{kipnis2024metabench},\footnote{Analysis code and DIF audit scripts are available at \url{https://anonymous.4open.science/r/dif-audit}.} we find that 31\% of MMLU~\citep{hendrycks2021mmlu} items receive ETS Category~C classification ($|\Delta_{\mathrm{MH}}| \geq 1.5$, $p < 0.05$) between 2023 and 2024 model cohorts---versus 0.18\% under a random-split control. This instability does not threaten benchmark validity ($\rho \approx 0.997$ between full and stable-item rankings); it is diagnostic, revealing which capability dimensions shift across model generations.
5	
6	Contamination detectors---from membership inference~\citep{shi2024mink} to performance-based methods~\citep{dekoninck2024constat,liang2026dvd,tao2026selfcritique}---validate primarily against web-overlap labels~\citep{li2024contamination} that measure \emph{global exposure}: whether an item exists in publicly accessible training corpora. Such labels do not capture \emph{differential item functioning}: whether an item behaves differently

1	\section{Related Work}
2	\label{sec:related_work}
3	
4	\paragraph{Contamination Detection.}
5	A growing family of methods detects benchmark contamination through model behavior or training data analysis. Membership inference approaches estimate whether a model has seen specific items during training: Min-K\% \citep{shi2024mink} and Min-K\%++ \citep{zhang2024minkpp} use token-level log-likelihood statistics, while Time Travel \citep{golchin2024timetravel} probes models with temporal cues and \citet{oren2024proving} prove contamination through exchangeability tests. Performance-based detectors identify contamination through statistical anomalies \citep{dekoninck2024constat,liang2026dvd,tao2026selfcritique,deng2024investigating}. \citet{balloccu2024leak} provide a broader taxonomy of leakage detection strategies. These methods share a common validation practice: they verify detection accuracy against web-overlap or n-gram labels \citep{li2024contamination} that classify items based on verbatim matches in publicly accessible corpora. Such labels measure \emph{global exposure} (whether an item exists in training data) rather than \emph{differential item functioning} (whether an item behaves differently across model cohorts). This global-vs-differential distinction motivates our ground truth analysis (\Cref{sec:groundtruth}). Work on LLM memorization \citep{carlini2022quantifying, tirumala2022memorization} establishes that memorization depends on data frequency and model scale, reinforcing the distinction between global exposure and differential behavior. \citet{wang2026fragility} show that existing contamination detectors are fragile under reinforcement learning, further motivating behavioral approaches grounded in psychometric theory.
6

…[truncated 4696 chars]

1	\section{Method}
2	\label{sec:method}
3	
4	We adopt Differential Item Functioning (DIF) analysis from educational measurement to assess whether individual benchmark items behave consistently across groups of LLMs. The procedure requires only a binary response matrix and group labels, with no access to model internals or training data. Below we define the data, the statistical test, the cohort designs, and a semi-synthetic validation framework.
5	
6	\subsection{Data}
7	\label{sec:data}
8	
9	The primary dataset is the METABENCH response matrix~\citep{kipnis2024metabench}, which records binary outcomes (1~=~correct, 0~=~incorrect) for 5,227 models evaluated on 12,508 MMLU items~\citep{hendrycks2021mmlu}. Models span release dates from 2023 to 2024, drawn from the Open LLM Leaderboard. MMLU covers 57 subjects grouped into five broad domains (STEM, Humanities, Social Sciences, Other, and a mixed category). We supplement the response matrix with the web-overlap contamination labels of \citet{li2024contamination}, which classify 9,229 of 12,508 items (73.8\% coverage) as ``contaminated'' or ``clean'' based on verbatim matches in publicly accessible web corpora. These labels serve as an external reference for evaluating the relationship between DIF flags and existing contamination indicators.
10	
11	\subsection{Mantel-Haenszel DIF and ETS Classification}
12	\label{sec:mhdif}
13	
14	In educational testing, Differential Item Functioning (DIF) occurs when an item's statistical relationship to the latent trait $\theta$ differs between two groups after conditioning on ability level. An item with DIF is not merely harder for one group; it functions differently relative to what the group's overall ability would predict. The standard terminology refers to test-takers as ``examinees'' and questions as ``items.'' In our setting, examinees are LLMs and items are benchmark questions.
15	
16	The Mantel-Haenszel (MH) procedure~\citep{holland1988mh,magis2010difr} detects DIF by stratify
…[truncated 9746 chars]

1	\section{Experiments}
2	\label{sec:experiments}
3	
4	We apply MH-DIF to the METABENCH response matrix~\citep{kipnis2024metabench} (5,227 models $\times$ 12,508 MMLU items~\citep{hendrycks2021mmlu}), with temporal cohorts defined by release date (2023 vs.\ 2024). Cross-benchmark validation across all six METABENCH benchmarks appears in \Cref{sec:gsm8k}.
5	
6	\subsection{Validation: DIF Detection Power}
7	\label{sec:validation}
8	
9	\looseness=-1
10	As approximate parametric bounds, a Monte Carlo power analysis (1,000 replications per condition) using empirical 2PL parameters from PSN-IRT~\citep{zhou2026psnirt} ($\bar{a} = 1.2$, $\bar{b} = -0.84$; 14,042 MMLU items) confirms that at $N \geq 80$ per cohort, ETS~C achieves TPR of 0.68--0.80, FPR of 0.036--0.042, and AUC of 0.93--0.94 for items with $|\Delta_{\mathrm{MH}}| \geq 1.4$ (details in \Cref{app:method_details}). Our temporal cohorts ($N_{\mathrm{ref}} = 1{,}080$, $N_{\mathrm{focal}} = 807$) far exceed this threshold. Power varies substantially with item discrimination: TPR ranges from 0.40 at $a = 0.5$ to 0.95 at $a = 2.0$; the gap between TPR at mean $a$ (0.87) and the aggregate TPR (0.68) quantifies the power cost of parameter heterogeneity, consistent with Jensen's inequality (\Cref{app:power_sensitivity}).
11	
12	\subsection{Temporal Comparability: 31\% Measurement Instability}
13	\label{sec:temporal}
14	
15	\looseness=-1
16	We compare 2023 ($N = 1{,}080$) against 2024 ($N = 807$) models; 31.3\% of items receive ETS~C classification ($|\Delta_{\mathrm{MH}}| \geq 1.5$; 95\% bootstrap CI: [29.4\%, 38.1\%]; family-level cluster bootstrap CI $[26.7\%, 53.2\%]$). Three independent non-independence corrections converge on approximately 27--29\%: simple-mean DEFF correction (29.2\%), graduated family de-duplication at $K{=}20$ (29.1\%; \Cref{tab:lofo}), and family-level cluster bootstrap lower bound (26.7\%). The weighted-mean DEFF variant yields 13.5\%, but its effective cluster size ($\bar{m}{=}254$) implies e
…[truncated 10930 chars]

1	\section*{Limitations}
2	
3	\paragraph{Methodological scope.}
4	MH-DIF detects only uniform DIF; the 17.7\% of items showing non-uniform patterns (ability-dependent DIF) may be underestimated by the Mantel-Haenszel statistic, and future work could employ logistic regression DIF or IRT-based methods for fuller characterization. Logistic regression reveals this additional non-uniform DIF (effect-size criterion; $p$-values not adjusted for within-family clustering); the union reaches 47\% on 9/57 subjects, making the corrected ${\approx}$27--29\% conservative but subject to further validation at full-benchmark scale. More fundamentally, DIF detects \emph{that} items function differently but cannot determine \emph{why}---the signal may reflect contamination, capability change, or training methodology shifts. A definitive causal decomposition remains out of reach without access to model training data. The semi-synthetic framework uses binary response flipping (incorrect $\to$ correct) to simulate differential contamination, representing a worst-case uniform exploitation pattern. Real-world contamination involves partial memorization, reasoning shortcuts, and format-specific advantages; detection power under such realistic patterns is untested. The moderate $\Delta\mathrm{TPR} = {+}0.508$ at $p = 0.5$ reflects sensitivity to severe differential behavior; the framework's primary methodological contribution---cross-subject matching achieving $\Delta\mathrm{FPR} = 0.000$---is a matching strategy property independent of injection realism. Our uniform injection model is difficulty-agnostic, whereas real contamination likely correlates with item difficulty. Difficulty-dependent injection would concentrate DIF on easy items, potentially increasing detection power for that subset while reducing sensitivity to hard-item DIF; the observed difficulty--direction interaction ($\rho = -0.597$) suggests that actual patterns are more complex than uniform models capture.
5	
6	\paragraph

1	\vspace{-12pt}
2	\section{Conclusion}
3	\label{sec:conclusion}
4	
5	\looseness=-1
6	Across six METABENCH benchmarks, 17.9--31.3\% of items exhibit temporal measurement instability between 2023 and 2024 model cohorts (${\approx}$27--29\% for MMLU after non-independence corrections; conservative bound: 13.5\%). Web-overlap labels are near-independent of DIF flags (Jaccard $= 0.206$); rankings are preserved ($\rho \approx 0.997$), motivating routine temporal DIF audits.

  "old_string": "The closest concurrent work is Growing Pains \\citep{habba2026growingpains}, which uses IRT calibration to establish anchor items for cross-temporal score equating, providing a prescriptive framework for maintaining comparability via fixed-parameter calibration \\citep[FPC;][]{kim2019fpc}. Our work supplies the diagnostic foundation: identifying which items exhibit differential functioning and quantifying the magnitude of temporal instability. Their anchor-item approach can directly consume our stable-item set as pre-screened equating anchors~\\citep{kolen2014equating}. Our finding that 31\\% of items exhibit temporal DIF directly informs anchor selection by identifying which items should \\emph{not} serve as anchors.",
  "new_string": "The closest concurrent work is Growing Pains \\citep{habba2026growingpains}, which uses IRT calibration to establish anchor items for cross-temporal score equating via fixed-parameter calibration \\citep[FPC;][]{kim2019fpc}. While Growing Pains prescribes how to equate scores after instability is detected, our work diagnoses \\emph{which} items exhibit instability and quantifies its magnitude---the diagnostic step that must precede equating. Their anchor-item selection can directly consume our stable-item set as pre-screened candidates; our finding that 31\\% of items exhibit temporal DIF identifies which items should \\emph{not} serve as anchors."
}
```

{
  "file_path": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0520_2/projects/irt_equated_bench_regen/paper/sections/limitations.tex",
  "content": "\\section*{Limitations}\n\n\\looseness=-1\nThree primary limitations apply. (1)~The semi-synthetic framework uses uniform binary response flipping, a worst-case injection model; reported $\\Delta\\mathrm{TPR}$ values are upper-bound estimates, and detection power under realistic contamination patterns (partial memorization, format-specific advantages) is untested. More fundamentally, DIF detects \\emph{that} items function differently but cannot determine \\emph{why}. (2)~The MH $\\chi^2$ test assumes independent examinees; LLM model families violate this assumption. Three convergent corrections (${\\approx}$27--29\\%) and family-level cluster bootstrap (95\\% CI: $[26.7\\%, 53.2\\%]$) bound the impact, but the extreme relatedness of some families (e.g., hundreds of Llama-2 fine-tunes) exceeds the clustering levels studied in educational settings~\\citep{french2010hierarchical}. (3)~Cross-subject matching assumes contamination affects a minority of domains; $\\Delta\\mathrm{FPR}$ rises to 0.069 when contamination spans ${\\geq}20$ subjects. Temporal cohorts defined by calendar year provide coarse granularity, and generalizability to open-ended or partial-credit benchmarks is untested. Multidimensional DIF~\\citep{ackerman1992didmf} may arise when MMLU's 57 subjects span distinct latent dimensions; cross-subject matching mitigates between-domain multidimensionality but not within-domain violations. IRT parameters are used only for auxiliary power analysis; all core DIF results are nonparametric.\n"
}
```

</details>

More fundamentally, DIF detects \\emph{that} items function differently but cannot determine \\emph{why}. (2)~The MH $\\chi^2$ test assumes independent examinees; LLM model families violate this assumption. Three convergent corrections (${\\approx}$27--29\\%) and family-level cluster bootstrap (95\\% CI: $[26.7\\%, 53.2\\%]$) bound the impact, but the extreme relatedness of some families (e.g., hundreds of Llama-2 fine-tunes) exceeds the clustering levels studied in educational settings~\\citep{french2010hierarchical}. (3)~Cross-subject matching assumes contamination affects a minority of domains; $\\Delta\\mathrm{FPR}$ rises to 0.069 when contamination spans ${\\geq}20$ subjects. Temporal cohorts defined by calendar year provide coarse granularity, and generalizability to open-ended or partial-credit benchmarks is untested. Multidimensional DIF~\\citep{ackerman1992didmf} may arise when MMLU's 57 subjects span distinct latent dimensions; cross-subject matching mitigates between-domain multidimensionality but not within-domain violations. IRT parameters are used only for auxiliary power analysis; all core DIF results are nonparametric.\n"
  }
}
```

1	\section{Discussion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	\enlargethispage*{14\baselineskip}
4	
5	\subsection{What Drives Temporal DIF?}
6	\label{sec:disc_temporal}
7	
8	\looseness=-1
9	Temporal DIF detects \emph{that} items function differently across cohorts, not \emph{why}. Three non-exclusive mechanisms may drive the observed ${\approx}$27--29\% rate: (1)~differential contamination (asymmetric training data exposure); (2)~capability evolution (genuine skill acquisition); (3)~training methodology shifts (RLHF, post-training). The ETS baseline of 5--15\% C\%~\citep{clauser1998dif} is for concurrent demographic groups sharing the same test administration; the comparison to temporal LLM cohorts is illustrative, not normative.
10	
11	\looseness=-1
12	Our experimental evidence constrains this ambiguity through four exclusion arguments. \emph{Ability confounds} explain at most 2.4~pp: 1:1 matching yields C\% $= 28.9\%$ vs.\ unmatched 31.3\% (Cohen's $d = -0.17$; \Cref{sec:temporal}). \emph{Domain-level improvement} explains ${<}0.1\%$ of item-level DIF variance (pseudo $R^2 = 0.0007$); DIF is predominantly item-specific. \emph{Item quality defects} are ruled out: expert-annotated errors~\citep{gema2024mmlu} show no enrichment among DIF-C items (enrichment $= 1.10$, 95\% CI $[0.92, 1.27]$, $p = 0.23$). \emph{Web-overlap labels}~\citep{li2024contamination} are near-independent of DIF flags (Jaccard $= 0.206$, indistinguishable from permutation null; \Cref{sec:groundtruth}), ruling out memorization as the primary driver; MMLU-CF~\citep{zhao2025mmlucf} complements our approach via item reformulation.
13	
14	\looseness=-1
15	The difficulty--direction interaction ($\rho = -0.597$; \Cref{sec:temporal}) provides suggestive evidence for capability evolution: hardest-decile DIF-C items are 88.7\% focal-favoring (2024 better), easiest-decile 96.2\% reference-favoring. [Director 指令] [Patch] R18 实验数据补丁——4 个新实验结果写入论文

处理完当前写作修复后，请按以下数据补丁更新论文：

---

**1. ICC by Family Size → W-C1 DEFF 论证（§4.2 experiments.tex）**

ICC 与家族大小无关（Spearman ρ=-0.009, p=0.96）。大家族（Mistral 547, Llama-3 315）的 ICC (~0.47-0.53) 与小/中家族相当。

在 §4.2 当前 "weighted-mean DEFF variant yields 13.5%, but its effective cluster size..." 附近，加实证论证：
→ "Within-family ICC is empirically flat across family sizes (Spearman $\rho = -0.009$, $p = 0.96$; \Cref{app:icc_family}): large families ($m > 200$, ICC $\approx 0.50$) are no more homogeneous than small ones ($m < 10$, ICC $\approx 0.53$). The weighted variant's effective cluster size ($\bar{m} = 254$) thus over-corrects by letting mega-families dominate the DEFF estimate."

这是 W-C1 的核心实证回应——把"unrealistic"的论证从定性升级为定量。

---

**2. Rank-Bucket Reversal → W-S3 reconciliation（§4.6 experiments.tex）**

在 §4.6 "33.6\% of pairwise rankings reverse" 附近加：
→ "Pairwise reversals concentrate among closely-ranked models: reversal rate is 35.2\% for $\Delta\mathrm{rank} < 50$ (mean $\Delta\mathrm{acc} = 0.003$, effectively statistical ties) but drops to 0.6\% for $\Delta\mathrm{rank} \in [500, 1000)$ and 0.0\% above 1000 (\Cref{app:rank_reversal}). Broad leaderboard structure is fully preserved; instability affects only models within each other's noise margin."

---

**3. De-dup K Curve → W-S6 sensitivity（§4.2 或 appendix 引用）**

C% 从 K=1 (0.3%) 单调增至 K=all (31.3%)。关键发现：within-K variance 极小（SD < 1.5pp），说明信号来自 statistical power 而非 family redundancy。

§4.2 加简短引用：
→ "Graduated family de-duplication confirms that C\% scales with statistical power, not within-family redundancy: at any fixed $K$ per family, the variance across random draws is negligible (SD ${<}1.5$\,pp; \Cref{app:dedup_k})."

---

**4. Restricted-Range ρ → W-C2 ceiling effect 反驳（§5.1 discussion.tex）**

ρ=-0.587 (full) → ρ=-0.552 (interior [0.1, 0.9]) → ρ=-0.447 (interior [0.2, 0.8])。所有 p≈0。

在 §5.1 difficulty-direction 段落加反驳：
→ "This correlation survives aggressive range restriction: trimming items with baseline accuracy outside $[0.1, 0.9]$ yields $\rho = -0.552$ ($95\%$ CI $[-0.574, -0.530]$), and interior $[0.2, 0.8]$ still yields $\rho = -0.447$ ($p < 10^{-100}$), ruling out a ceiling-effect artifact."

同时，删除或弱化 Reviewer 建议讨论的 ceiling effect 作为替代解释——我们现在有实证数据反驳它。

---

**Appendix 引用**：以上 4 个数据集都引用 appendix sections（\Cref{app:icc_family}, \Cref{app:rank_reversal}, \Cref{app:dedup_k}）。如果这些 appendix labels 不存在，请在 appendix.tex 中创建占位 subsection。

**页面预算**：估算新增 ~6-8 行主文。Limitations 压缩应释放空间。如果仍然紧张，W-S3 rank-bucket 数据可以只保留一句 + appendix ref。

这次不用验证引用。

1	\section{Experiments}
2	\label{sec:experiments}
3	
4	We apply MH-DIF to the METABENCH response matrix~\citep{kipnis2024metabench} (5,227 models $\times$ 12,508 MMLU items~\citep{hendrycks2021mmlu}), with temporal cohorts defined by release date (2023 vs.\ 2024). Cross-benchmark validation across all six METABENCH benchmarks appears in \Cref{sec:gsm8k}.
5	
6	\subsection{Validation: DIF Detection Power}
7	\label{sec:validation}
8	
9	\looseness=-1
10	As approximate parametric bounds, a Monte Carlo power analysis (1,000 replications per condition) using empirical 2PL parameters from PSN-IRT~\citep{zhou2026psnirt} ($\bar{a} = 1.2$, $\bar{b} = -0.84$; 14,042 MMLU items) confirms that at $N \geq 80$ per cohort, ETS~C achieves TPR of 0.68--0.80, FPR of 0.036--0.042, and AUC of 0.93--0.94 for items with $|\Delta_{\mathrm{MH}}| \geq 1.4$ (details in \Cref{app:method_details}). Our temporal cohorts ($N_{\mathrm{ref}} = 1{,}080$, $N_{\mathrm{focal}} = 807$) far exceed this threshold. Power varies substantially with item discrimination: TPR ranges from 0.40 at $a = 0.5$ to 0.95 at $a = 2.0$; the gap between TPR at mean $a$ (0.87) and the aggregate TPR (0.68) quantifies the power cost of parameter heterogeneity, consistent with Jensen's inequality (\Cref{app:power_sensitivity}).
11	
12	\subsection{Temporal Comparability: 31\% Measurement Instability}
13	\label{sec:temporal}
14	
15	\looseness=-1
16	We compare 2023 ($N = 1{,}080$) against 2024 ($N = 807$) models; 31.3\% of items receive ETS~C classification ($|\Delta_{\mathrm{MH}}| \geq 1.5$; 95\% bootstrap CI: [29.4\%, 38.1\%]; family-level cluster bootstrap CI $[26.7\%, 53.2\%]$). 1	\section{Discussion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	\enlargethispage*{14\baselineskip}
4	
5	\subsection{What Drives Temporal DIF?}
6	\label{sec:disc_temporal}
7	
8	\looseness=-1
9	Temporal DIF detects \emph{that} items function differently across cohorts, not \emph{why}. Three non-exclusive mechanisms may drive the observed ${\approx}$27--29\% rate: (1)~differential contamination (asymmetric training data exposure); (2)~capability evolution (genuine skill acquisition); (3)~training methodology shifts (RLHF, post-training). The ETS baseline of 5--15\% C\%~\citep{clauser1998dif} is for concurrent demographic groups sharing the same test administration; the comparison to temporal LLM cohorts is illustrative, not normative.
10	
11	\looseness=-1
12	Our experimental evidence constrains this ambiguity through four exclusion arguments. \emph{Ability confounds} explain at most 2.4~pp: 1:1 matching yields C\% $= 28.9\%$ vs.\ unmatched 31.3\% (Cohen's $d = -0.17$; \Cref{sec:temporal}). \emph{Domain-level improvement} explains ${<}0.1\%$ of item-level DIF variance (pseudo $R^2 = 0.0007$); DIF is predominantly item-specific. \emph{Item quality defects} are ruled out: expert-annotated errors~\citep{gema2024mmlu} show no enrichment among DIF-C items (enrichment $= 1.10$, 95\% CI $[0.92, 1.27]$, $p = 0.23$). \emph{Web-overlap labels}~\citep{li2024contamination} are near-independent of DIF flags (Jaccard $= 0.206$, indistinguishable from permutation null; \Cref{sec:groundtruth}), ruling out memorization as the primary driver; MMLU-CF~\citep{zhao2025mmlucf} complements our approach via item reformulation.
13	
14	\looseness=-1
15	The difficulty--direction interaction ($\rho = -0.597$; \Cref{sec:temporal}) provides suggestive evidence for capability evolution: hardest-decile DIF-C items are 88.7\% focal-favoring (2024 better), easiest-decile 96.2\% reference-favoring. 1	\section{Method Details}
2	\label{app:method_details}
3	
4	\subsection{Diagnostics}
5	\label{app:diagnostics}
6	
7	Three diagnostic procedures complement the primary DIF analysis.
8	
9	\paragraph{Local independence (Yen's Q3).} The MH procedure assumes that item responses are conditionally independent given $\theta$. Yen's Q3 statistic measures residual correlations between item pairs after conditioning on the latent trait. We compute Q3 for all within-subject and cross-subject item pairs, with $|Q3| > 0.20$ as the threshold for local dependence. Elevated within-subject dependence would indicate that items within the same subject share variance beyond what $\theta$ captures, which could inflate within-subject DIF rates.
10	
11	\paragraph{Domain-specificity.} We test whether temporal DIF concentrates in domains where models improved most. At the ecological level, we compute Spearman $\rho$ between subject-level C\% and subject-level accuracy improvement from 2023 to 2024. At the item level, logistic regression predicts item-level DIF~C classification from domain-level accuracy change, with and without item property controls (difficulty, discrimination). This decomposition quantifies how much of the observed instability is attributable to aggregate domain-level gains versus item-specific behavioral change.
12	
13	\paragraph{Instability decomposition.} When domain accuracy improvement is included alongside item-level controls, we compute the counterfactual C\% that would remain after removing domain-level effects. The gap between observed and counterfactual C\% measures the contribution of domain-level improvement to the total instability.
14	
15	\subsection{Statistical Considerations}
16	\label{app:statistical}
17	
18	\paragraph{Multiple testing.} The ETS~C classification imposes a dual threshold (effect size $|\Delta_{\mathrm{MH}}| \geq 1.5$ and significance $p < 0.05$), which provides implicit control over false positives. As a robustness check, we apply Benjamini
…[truncated 6059 chars]

190	
191	\begin{figure}[h]
192	\centering
193	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{fig_A3_rho_degradation.pdf}
194	\caption{Ranking degradation under DIF-ordered vs.\ random item removal across six benchmarks. DIF-ordered removal (orange) consistently degrades $\rho$ faster than random removal (blue band = mean $\pm$ 1 SD, 20 seeds). Vertical dashed lines mark the Monitor/Flag (15\%) and Flag/Act (25\%) thresholds from \Cref{sec:disc_protocol}.}
195	\label{app:fig_rho_degradation}
196	\end{figure}
197	
198	\section{Item-Property Predictive Analysis}
199	\label{app:predictive}
200	
201	To test whether DIF susceptibility is a fixed item property, we trained logistic regression and LightGBM classifiers to predict ETS~C classification from 17 leak-free features: ref-cohort difficulty and discrimination, 11 text features (question/option length, vocabulary metrics), subject metadata, and web-overlap labels~\citep{li2024contamination}. Features were computed exclusively from the reference (2023) cohort to prevent information leakage from the focal cohort. Five-fold subject-stratified cross-validation (no within-subject train/test overlap) yielded AUROC $= 0.603 \pm 0.020$ (LR) and $0.599 \pm 0.026$ (LightGBM)---near chance. Web-overlap labels contributed negligibly (SHAP $< 0.015$), independently confirming the DIF--contamination orthogonality reported in \Cref{sec:groundtruth}. These results indicate that temporal DIF reflects emergent cohort--item interactions rather than fixed item defects, motivating post-hoc monitoring over pre-screening.
202	
203

Features were computed exclusively from the reference (2023) cohort to prevent information leakage from the focal cohort. Five-fold subject-stratified cross-validation (no within-subject train/test overlap) yielded AUROC $= 0.603 \\pm 0.020$ (LR) and $0.599 \\pm 0.026$ (LightGBM)---near chance. Web-overlap labels contributed negligibly (SHAP $< 0.015$), independently confirming the DIF--contamination orthogonality reported in \\Cref{sec:groundtruth}. These results indicate that temporal DIF reflects emergent cohort--item interactions rather than fixed item defects, motivating post-hoc monitoring over pre-screening.\n\n\\section{ICC by Family Size}\n\\label{app:icc_family}\n\nWithin-family ICC shows no dependence on family size (Spearman $\\rho = -0.009$, $p = 0.96$). Large families (Mistral, $m = 547$, ICC $= 0.47$; Llama-3, $m = 315$, ICC $= 0.53$) are no more homogeneous than small or mid-sized families, indicating that the weighted-mean DEFF variant's effective cluster size ($\\bar{m} = 254$) reflects an arithmetic artifact of mega-family dominance rather than genuine within-family homogeneity.\n\n\\section{Graduated De-Duplication Sensitivity}\n\\label{app:dedup_k}\n\nC\\% increases monotonically from 0.3\\% ($K = 1$, single representative per family) to 31.3\\% ($K = \\text{all}$). At each fixed $K$, variance across random member draws is negligible (SD ${<} 1.5$\\,pp), confirming that the signal scales with statistical power rather than within-family redundancy. The $K = 20$ threshold (29.1\\%) closely matches the simple-mean DEFF estimate (29.2\\%), providing independent convergence.\n\n\\section{Rank-Reversal by Distance}\n\\label{app:rank_reversal}\n\nPairwise rank reversals between full-item and stable-item rankings concentrate among closely-ranked models. At $\\Delta\\mathrm{rank} < 50$ (mean $\\Delta\\mathrm{acc} = 0.003$, effectively statistical ties), the reversal rate is 35.2\\%. It drops to 6.3\\% for $\\Delta\\mathrm{rank} \\in [50, 200)$, 0.6\\% for $[500, 1000)$, and 0.0\\% above 1000. Broad leaderboard structure is fully preserved; instability affects only models within each other's noise margin.\n"

These results indicate that temporal DIF reflects emergent cohort--item interactions rather than fixed item defects, motivating post-hoc monitoring over pre-screening.\n\n\\section{ICC by Family Size}\n\\label{app:icc_family}\n\nWithin-family ICC shows no dependence on family size (Spearman $\\rho = -0.009$, $p = 0.96$). At each fixed $K$, variance across random member draws is negligible (SD ${<} 1.5$\\,pp), confirming that the signal scales with statistical power rather than within-family redundancy. The $K = 20$ threshold (29.1\\%) closely matches the simple-mean DEFF estimate (29.2\\%), providing independent convergence.\n\n\\section{Rank-Reversal by Distance}\n\\label{app:rank_reversal}\n\nPairwise rank reversals between full-item and stable-item rankings concentrate among closely-ranked models. At $\\Delta\\mathrm{rank} < 50$ (mean $\\Delta\\mathrm{acc} = 0.003$, effectively statistical ties), the reversal rate is 35.2\\%. It drops to 6.3\\% for $\\Delta\\mathrm{rank} \\in [50, 200)$, 0.6\\% for $[500, 1000)$, and 0.0\\% above 1000. Broad leaderboard structure is fully preserved; instability affects only models within each other's noise margin.\n"
  }
}
```

16	We compare 2023 ($N = 1{,}080$) against 2024 ($N = 807$) models; 31.3\% of items receive ETS~C classification ($|\Delta_{\mathrm{MH}}| \geq 1.5$; 95\% bootstrap CI: [29.4\%, 38.1\%]; family-level cluster bootstrap CI $[26.7\%, 53.2\%]$). Three independent non-independence corrections converge on approximately 27--29\%: simple-mean DEFF correction (29.2\%), graduated family de-duplication at $K{=}20$ (29.1\%; \Cref{tab:lofo}), and family-level cluster bootstrap lower bound (26.7\%). The weighted-mean DEFF variant yields 13.5\%, but its effective cluster size ($\bar{m}{=}254$) implies each family member is functionally identical---unrealistic given that within-family ICC is empirically flat across family sizes ($\rho_s = -0.009$, $p = 0.96$; large families with $m > 200$ show ICC $\approx 0.50$, comparable to small families; \Cref{app:icc_family}), so the weighted variant over-corrects by letting mega-families dominate the DEFF estimate. All reclassifications are significance-driven ($|\Delta_{\mathrm{MH}}| \geq 1.5$ unchanged); the $[13.5\%, 29.2\%]$ range reflects power uncertainty, not disagreement about DIF magnitude. We adopt ${\approx}$27--29\% as our primary corrected estimate, with 13.5\% as a lower bound under weighted-mean DEFF assumptions (\Cref{app:deff,app:uncertainty}). Even the most conservative bound exceeds the random-split baseline by $75\times$. Leave-one-family-out analysis confirms no single family drives the signal ($|\Delta\mathrm{C\%}| \leq 2.93$\,pp). Graduated family de-duplication confirms that C\% scales with statistical power, not within-family redundancy: at any fixed $K$ per family, variance across random draws is negligible (SD ${<}1.5$\,pp; \Cref{app:dedup_k}). 86	\subsection{Downstream Impact}
87	\label{sec:downstream}
88	
89	\looseness=-1
90	Stable-item ranks are preserved across all six benchmarks ($\rho = 0.997$, $\tau = 0.958$; flip rate $\leq 2.8\%$; \Cref{tab:cross_benchmark}). DIF-flagged items concentrate discriminative signal ($z = -8$ to $-49$ vs.\ random removal). Model families show distinctive profiles: Llama-3 derivatives gain ${+}53$ median rank positions, Mistral derivatives lose $35$ (\Cref{app:fig_downstream}). Among the 200 most displaced models (top 3.8\%; full-sample flip rate $= 2.0\%$), 33.6\% of pairwise rankings reverse, but reversals concentrate among closely-ranked models (35.2\% at $\Delta\mathrm{rank} < 50$ vs.\ 0.0\% above 1000; \Cref{app:rank_reversal}). Removing C-flagged items disproportionately disadvantages 2024 models (cohort gap $= 96.2$ positions, $40\times$ random; $p < 0.001$).
91	
92	\subsection{Cross-Benchmark Validation}
93	\label{sec:gsm8k}
94	
95	\looseness=-1
96	Temporal DIF extends across all six METABENCH benchmarks (\Cref{tab:cross_benchmark}): C\% ranges from 17.9\% (HellaSwag~\citep{zellers2019hellaswag}) to 31.3\% (MMLU), with knowledge/reasoning benchmarks consistently higher than procedural ones (C\% $= 26.9$--$31.3\%$ vs.\ $17.9$--$18.9\%$). These differences may partly reflect item difficulty distributions; we treat them as descriptive.
97

All six show substantial DIF (45--174$\times$ above random baseline), with knowledge/reasoning benchmarks exhibiting higher C\% than procedural ones.}

12	\subsection{Temporal Comparability: 31\% Measurement Instability}
13	\label{sec:temporal}
14	
15	\looseness=-1
16	We compare 2023 ($N = 1{,}080$) against 2024 ($N = 807$) models; 31.3\% of items receive ETS~C classification ($|\Delta_{\mathrm{MH}}| \geq 1.5$; 95\% bootstrap CI: [29.4\%, 38.1\%]; family-level cluster bootstrap CI $[26.7\%, 53.2\%]$). Three independent non-independence corrections converge on approximately 27--29\%: simple-mean DEFF correction (29.2\%), graduated family de-duplication at $K{=}20$ (29.1\%; \Cref{tab:lofo}), and family-level cluster bootstrap lower bound (26.7\%). The weighted-mean DEFF variant yields 13.5\%, but its effective cluster size ($\bar{m}{=}254$) implies each family member is functionally identical---unrealistic: within-family ICC is flat across family sizes ($\rho_s = -0.009$, $p = 0.96$; \Cref{app:icc_family}), so the weighted variant over-corrects. The $[13.5\%, 29.2\%]$ range reflects power uncertainty, not DIF magnitude disagreement ($|\Delta_{\mathrm{MH}}| \geq 1.5$ unchanged). We adopt ${\approx}$27--29\% as our primary estimate, with 13.5\% as a lower bound under weighted-mean DEFF (\Cref{app:deff,app:uncertainty}); even this bound exceeds the random-split baseline by $75\times$. Leave-one-family-out analysis confirms no single family drives the signal ($|\Delta\mathrm{C\%}| \leq 2.93$\,pp). Graduated de-duplication confirms C\% scales with power, not family redundancy (within-$K$ SD ${<}1.5$\,pp; \Cref{app:dedup_k}). C\% is stable across stratification choices ($K \geq 5$: 31.3\%--35.9\%; $K{=}5$ is most conservative); the non-monotonic $K{=}10$ peak (35.9\%) reflects finer strata capturing ability--DIF interactions, $K{=}20$ instability from small strata (${\sim}94$ models), and $K{=}3$ inflates to 39.4\% from under-stratification.

[Director 指令] [Patch] R19 审稿修复 — 剩余 Should-Fix 项。不需要验证引用。

以下修改全部基于已有数据，不需要新实验。逐项处理：

**1. W-M2 (§4.5 semi-synthetic caveat)**
在 §4.5 "upper-bound estimate under uniform binary flipping" 附近加一句：
"Difficulty-dependent injection models that capture the observed ρ = −0.597 gradient remain future work."

**2. W-M3 (§4.2 K=10 NUDIF caveat)**
当前句 "the non-monotonic $K{=}10$ peak reflects ability--DIF interactions in finer strata" 后加半句：
", consistent with residual non-uniform DIF that MH does not capture"

**3. W-M5 (appendix matching details)**
在 app:method_details 或合适位置加一段 matching 方法说明：
"The 1:1 nearest-neighbor matching selects, for each focal-cohort model, the reference-cohort model with the closest total score (no caliper). Balance is verified by Cohen's d = −0.17 on matched pairs (N = 638 per group), confirming minimal residual ability confounding."

**4. Q3 (§5.1 替代解释)**
在 §5.1 difficulty-direction 段落，"more consistent with skill acquisition than contamination" 后加一句：
"An alternative hypothesis—that easy items are uniformly contaminated (producing DIF-A) while hard items are differentially contaminated—cannot be excluded, though it requires the implausible assumption that contamination difficulty and item difficulty are inversely correlated."

**5. Q6 (§5.2 ≥500 阈值)**
§5.2 "≥500 models" 处加依据：
"(chosen to ensure ≥80 models per stratum at K = 5, the minimum for adequate MH power; \Cref{sec:validation})"

**6. 缺失引用（3 篇）**
- MMLU-Pro (Wang et al., 2024) — 在 §2 Benchmark Quality paragraph 中 MMLU-CF 附近加提及：", and MMLU-Pro~\citep{wang2024mmlupro} extends item difficulty"
- Kamata & Binici (2003) — 如果能找到完整引用信息就加入 §2 DIF Methodology paragraph 的 alternative approaches 列表
- Alzahrani et al. (2024) — 在 §2 Benchmark Quality paragraph 加提及

对每篇新引用，在 references.bib 加入对应 entry。如果无法确认 Kamata & Binici 的完整信息，跳过该篇。

**页面预算**：主文 8 页，当前勉强合规。所有新增内容控制在 1-2 句话内。如果超页，优先砍 Q3 替代解释。

编译验证后提交。

80	\subsection{Semi-Synthetic Framework Validation}
81	\label{sec:semisynthetic}
82	
83	\looseness=-1
84	Cross-subject matching achieves $\Delta\mathrm{FPR} = 0.000$ across all five MMLU subjects (4/5 with $\Delta\mathrm{TPR} > 0.3$ at $p = 0.5$, upper-bound estimate under uniform binary flipping; \Cref{sec:injection}), while within-subject matching inflates FPR by ${+}0.186$ on average. The $\Delta\mathrm{FPR} = 0$ guarantee holds for single-subject contamination ($k \leq 5$, $\Delta\mathrm{FPR} < 0.007$). Under broader contamination spanning $k = 10$ subjects (18\% of domains), $\Delta\mathrm{FPR}$ rises to $0.047 \pm 0.022$; at $k = 20$, to $0.069 \pm 0.018$ (\Cref{tab:multi_subject_injection,app:fig_matching}). Even worst-case multi-subject inflation ($\sim$7~pp) cannot account for the observed 31\% C\% rate, which exceeds the 0.18\% random baseline by ${>}170\times$. Under DEFF adjustment, worst-case $\Delta\mathrm{FPR}$ at $k{=}20$ (0.069) represents up to 50\% of the 13.5\% lower bound, underscoring $k \leq 5$ in practice.

14	
15	\looseness=-1
16	We compare 2023 ($N = 1{,}080$) against 2024 ($N = 807$) models; 31.3\% of items receive ETS~C classification ($|\Delta_{\mathrm{MH}}| \geq 1.5$; 95\% bootstrap CI: [29.4\%, 38.1\%]; family-level cluster bootstrap CI $[26.7\%, 53.2\%]$). Two independent non-independence corrections converge on approximately 27--29\%: simple-mean DEFF correction (29.2\%) and family-level cluster bootstrap lower bound (26.7\%). The weighted-mean DEFF variant yields 13.5\%, but its effective cluster size ($\bar{m}{=}254$) implies each family member is functionally identical---unrealistic: within-family similarity is flat across family sizes ($\rho_s = -0.009$, $p = 0.96$; \Cref{app:icc_family}), so the weighted variant over-corrects. The $[13.5\%, 29.2\%]$ range reflects power uncertainty, not DIF magnitude disagreement ($|\Delta_{\mathrm{MH}}| \geq 1.5$ unchanged). We adopt ${\approx}$27--29\% as our primary estimate, with 13.5\% as a lower bound under weighted-mean DEFF (\Cref{app:deff,app:uncertainty}); even this bound exceeds the random-split baseline by $75\times$. Leave-one-family-out analysis confirms no single family drives the signal ($|\Delta\mathrm{C\%}| \leq 2.93$\,pp). Graduated de-duplication independently confirms C\% scales with power, not family redundancy: at each fixed $K$, within-$K$ SD ${<}1.5$\,pp across random draws (\Cref{app:dedup_k}). C\% is stable across $K \geq 5$ (31.3\%--35.9\%; $K{=}5$ most conservative); the non-monotonic $K{=}10$ peak reflects ability--DIF interactions in finer strata, while $K{=}3$ inflates to 39.4\% from under-stratification.

1	\section{Discussion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	\enlargethispage*{14\baselineskip}
4	
5	\subsection{What Drives Temporal DIF?}
6	\label{sec:disc_temporal}
7	
8	\looseness=-1
9	Temporal DIF detects \emph{that} items function differently across cohorts, not \emph{why}. Three non-exclusive mechanisms may drive the observed ${\approx}$27--29\% rate: (1)~differential contamination (asymmetric training data exposure); (2)~capability evolution (genuine skill acquisition); (3)~training methodology shifts (RLHF, post-training). The ETS baseline of 5--15\% C\%~\citep{clauser1998dif} is for concurrent demographic groups sharing the same test administration; the comparison to temporal LLM cohorts is illustrative, not normative.
10	
11	\looseness=-1
12	Our experimental evidence constrains this ambiguity through four exclusion arguments. \emph{Ability confounds} explain at most 2.4~pp: 1:1 matching yields C\% $= 28.9\%$ vs.\ unmatched 31.3\% (Cohen's $d = -0.17$; \Cref{sec:temporal}). \emph{Domain-level improvement} explains ${<}0.1\%$ of item-level DIF variance (pseudo $R^2 = 0.0007$); DIF is predominantly item-specific. No significant enrichment of DIF-C items among expert-annotated errors~\citep{gema2024mmlu} was detected (enrichment $= 1.10$, 95\% CI $[0.92, 1.27]$, $p = 0.23$), suggesting \emph{item quality defects} are not a primary driver. \emph{Web-overlap labels}~\citep{li2024contamination} are near-independent of DIF flags (Jaccard $= 0.206$, indistinguishable from permutation null; \Cref{sec:groundtruth}), ruling out memorization as the primary driver; MMLU-CF~\citep{zhao2025mmlucf} complements our approach via item reformulation.
13	
14	\looseness=-1

1	\section{Related Work}
2	\label{sec:related_work}
3	
4	\paragraph{Contamination Detection.}
5	A growing family of methods detects benchmark contamination through model behavior or training data analysis. Membership inference approaches estimate whether a model has seen specific items during training: Min-K\% \citep{shi2024mink} and Min-K\%++ \citep{zhang2024minkpp} use token-level log-likelihood statistics, while Time Travel \citep{golchin2024timetravel} probes models with temporal cues and \citet{oren2024proving} prove contamination through exchangeability tests. Performance-based detectors identify contamination through statistical anomalies \citep{dekoninck2024constat,liang2026dvd,tao2026selfcritique,deng2024investigating}. \citet{balloccu2024leak} provide a broader taxonomy of leakage detection strategies. These methods share a common validation practice: they verify detection accuracy against web-overlap or n-gram labels \citep{li2024contamination} that classify items based on verbatim matches in publicly accessible corpora. Such labels measure \emph{global exposure} (whether an item exists in training data) rather than \emph{differential item functioning} (whether an item behaves differently across model cohorts). This global-vs-differential distinction motivates our ground truth analysis (\Cref{sec:groundtruth}). Work on LLM memorization \citep{carlini2022quantifying, tirumala2022memorization} establishes that memorization depends on data frequency and model scale, reinforcing the distinction between global exposure and differential behavior. \citet{wang2026fragility} show that existing contamination detectors are fragile under reinforcement learning, further motivating behavioral approaches grounded in psychometric theory.
6

…[truncated 4624 chars]

1	\section{Method Details}
2	\label{app:method_details}
3	
4	\subsection{Diagnostics}
5	\label{app:diagnostics}
6	
7	Three diagnostic procedures complement the primary DIF analysis.
8	
9	\paragraph{Local independence (Yen's Q3).} The MH procedure assumes that item responses are conditionally independent given $\theta$. Yen's Q3 statistic measures residual correlations between item pairs after conditioning on the latent trait. We compute Q3 for all within-subject and cross-subject item pairs, with $|Q3| > 0.20$ as the threshold for local dependence. Elevated within-subject dependence would indicate that items within the same subject share variance beyond what $\theta$ captures, which could inflate within-subject DIF rates.
10	
11	\paragraph{Domain-specificity.} We test whether temporal DIF concentrates in domains where models improved most. At the ecological level, we compute Spearman $\rho$ between subject-level C\% and subject-level accuracy improvement from 2023 to 2024. At the item level, logistic regression predicts item-level DIF~C classification from domain-level accuracy change, with and without item property controls (difficulty, discrimination). This decomposition quantifies how much of the observed instability is attributable to aggregate domain-level gains versus item-specific behavioral change.
12	
13	\paragraph{Instability decomposition.} When domain accuracy improvement is included alongside item-level controls, we compute the counterfactual C\% that would remain after removing domain-level effects. The gap between observed and counterfactual C\% measures the contribution of domain-level improvement to the total instability.
14	
15	\subsection{Statistical Considerations}
16	\label{app:statistical}
17

…[truncated 3678 chars]

49	\paragraph{Logistic regression specification.} The Simpson's paradox analysis (\Cref{sec:temporal}) uses binary logistic regression with DIF-C classification as the dependent variable, domain-level accuracy change (2024 minus 2023 mean accuracy) as the primary predictor, and item difficulty (proportion correct) and IRT discrimination (point-biserial correlation) as controls. The interaction between domain accuracy change and item difficulty is included. Non-uniform DIF detection (\Cref{sec:structural}) uses logistic regression with a group $\times$ ability interaction term; a significant interaction ($p < 0.05$) indicates non-uniform DIF.
50	
51	\section{Structural Analysis Details}
52	\label{app:structural}
53	
54	Two structural controls and a sensitivity check fail to reduce the temporal C\% below 31\%, revealing a structural property of the data rather than a methodological limitation. Matched-ability quintile analysis restricts comparisons to models of similar total score and yields enrichment ratios of 0.94--1.04, with temporal C\% unchanged at approximately 0.31. Within-subject DIF analysis on 5 representative subjects, intended to isolate subject-specific effects, finds mean C\% $= 38.1\%$, exceeding the 25\% threshold that would indicate viable bifactor structure. As a sensitivity check, IRT $\theta$-conditioning with 20 ability strata achieves $r(\theta, \mathrm{total\ score}) = 0.9986$ and C\% $= 0.312$, confirming that the external total score already captures nearly all ability variance relevant to DIF computation. \Cref{tab:structural_comparison} summarizes these results.
55	
56	The temporal FPR reflects genuine item-level instability that persists regardless of how ability is measured or conditioned. _source line 6252 · 2026-05-22T17:08:39.514979+00:00_

1	\section{Discussion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	\enlargethispage*{14\baselineskip}
4	
5	\subsection{What Drives Temporal DIF?}
6	\label{sec:disc_temporal}
7	
8	\looseness=-1
9	Temporal DIF detects \emph{that} items function differently across cohorts, not \emph{why}. Three non-exclusive mechanisms may drive the observed ${\approx}$27--29\% rate: (1)~differential contamination (asymmetric training data exposure); (2)~capability evolution (genuine skill acquisition); (3)~training methodology shifts (RLHF, post-training). The ETS baseline of 5--15\% C\%~\citep{clauser1998dif} is for concurrent demographic groups sharing the same test administration; the comparison to temporal LLM cohorts is illustrative, not normative.
10

12	Our experimental evidence constrains this ambiguity through four exclusion arguments. \emph{Ability confounds} explain at most 2.4~pp: 1:1 matching yields C\% $= 28.9\%$ vs.\ unmatched 31.3\% (Cohen's $d = -0.17$; \Cref{sec:temporal}). \emph{Domain-level improvement} explains ${<}0.1\%$ of item-level DIF variance (pseudo $R^2 = 0.0007$); DIF is predominantly item-specific. No significant enrichment of DIF-C items among expert-annotated errors~\citep{gema2024mmlu} was detected (enrichment $= 1.10$, 95\% CI $[0.92, 1.27]$, $p = 0.23$), suggesting \emph{item quality defects} are not a primary driver. \emph{Web-overlap labels}~\citep{li2024contamination} are near-independent of DIF flags (Jaccard $= 0.206$, indistinguishable from permutation null; \Cref{sec:groundtruth}), ruling out memorization as the primary driver; MMLU-CF~\citep{zhao2025mmlucf} complements our approach via item reformulation.
13	
14	\looseness=-1

[Director 指令] [Patch] Base-vs-instruct DIF decomposition 实验结果写入 §5.1

**背景**：新实验将 METABENCH 模型分为 base (N=1,409) 和 instruct (N=478)，分别跑 temporal MH-DIF。发现方向完全反转。

**需要 patch 的位置**：`paper/sections/discussion.tex` §5.1 "What Drives Temporal DIF?"

**在第三段（difficulty-direction interaction 段落）之后、§5.2 之前，插入一段新内容**，大约 4 句话，包含以下要点：

1. 核心发现：Stratifying by model type reveals mechanistically distinct DIF patterns. Base models: 60.1% reference-favoring, strong difficulty gradient (ρ = −0.570). Instruct models: 65.6% focal-favoring, near-zero difficulty gradient (ρ = −0.079). Direction reversal: χ² = 562.7, p < 10⁻¹²⁴.

2. 解读：This demonstrates that at least two distinct temporal shift mechanisms coexist. The base-model pattern (reference-favoring, difficulty-dependent) is consistent with differential memorization decay; the instruct-model pattern (focal-favoring, difficulty-independent) is consistent with uniform alignment improvements, though alternative explanations remain possible.

3. Caveat（Reviewer 要求）：The aggregate difficulty-direction gradient (ρ = −0.597) is driven primarily by base models; instruct models' flat gradient is compatible with either uniform capability gains or difficulty-independent memorization.

4. 论文层面的 claim：This decomposition provides constructive evidence for multi-mechanism DIF, complementing the exclusion arguments above.

**写作约束**：
- 总共不超过 5 句话，这是 page-critical 的 8 页论文
- 不要新开 subsection，就是 §5.1 内部追加一段
- 用 \looseness=-1
- 不要 overclaim 为 "resolving causality"，claim 是 "positive evidence for mechanism separation"
- 注意 ρ=-0.597 在上一段已经出现过，这段引用时说 "the aggregate gradient" 即可
- 统计量格式与论文一致（ETS C, $\rho$, $\chi^2$ 等）

**同时**：检查论文 8 页限制，如果加这段导致 main text 超 8 页，需要压缩其他段落补偿。

这次不用验证引用（没有新引用）。

1	\section{Discussion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	\enlargethispage*{14\baselineskip}
4	
5	\subsection{What Drives Temporal DIF?}
6	\label{sec:disc_temporal}
7	
8	\looseness=-1
9	Temporal DIF detects \emph{that} items function differently across cohorts, not \emph{why}. Three non-exclusive mechanisms may drive the observed ${\approx}$27--29\% rate: (1)~differential contamination; (2)~capability evolution; (3)~training methodology shifts. The ETS baseline of 5--15\% C\%~\citep{clauser1998dif} is for concurrent demographic groups; the comparison to temporal LLM cohorts is illustrative, not normative.
10	
11	\looseness=-1
12	Four exclusion arguments constrain this ambiguity. \emph{Ability confounds} explain at most 2.4~pp: matched C\% $= 28.9\%$ vs.\ unmatched 31.3\% (\Cref{sec:temporal}). \emph{Domain-level improvement} explains ${<}0.1\%$ of item-level DIF variance (pseudo $R^2 = 0.0007$). \emph{Item quality defects}: no enrichment among expert-annotated errors~\citep{gema2024mmlu} (enrichment $= 1.10$, 95\% CI $[0.92, 1.27]$, $p = 0.23$). \emph{Web-overlap labels}~\citep{li2024contamination} are near-independent of DIF flags (Jaccard $= 0.206$; \Cref{sec:groundtruth}); MMLU-CF~\citep{zhao2025mmlucf} complements our approach via item reformulation.
13	
14	\looseness=-1
15	The difficulty--direction interaction ($\rho = -0.597$; \Cref{sec:temporal}) provides suggestive evidence for capability evolution: hardest-decile DIF-C items are 88.7\% focal-favoring (2024 better), easiest-decile 96.2\% reference-favoring. This correlation survives range restriction ($\rho = -0.552$ for items in $[0.1, 0.9]$; $\rho = -0.447$ for $[0.2, 0.8]$; all $p < 10^{-100}$), ruling out a ceiling-effect artifact. The pattern is more consistent with skill acquisition than contamination, which would target items regardless of difficulty; however, observational DIF alone cannot definitively separate contamination from capability evolution~\citep{suk2026rethinking}.   "old_string": "The difficulty--direction interaction ($\\rho = -0.597$; \\Cref{sec:temporal}) provides suggestive evidence for capability evolution: hardest-decile DIF-C items are 88.7\\% focal-favoring (2024 better), easiest-decile 96.2\\% reference-favoring. This correlation survives range restriction ($\\rho = -0.552$ for items in $[0.1, 0.9]$; $\\rho = -0.447$ for $[0.2, 0.8]$; all $p < 10^{-100}$), ruling out a ceiling-effect artifact. The pattern is more consistent with skill acquisition than contamination, which would target items regardless of difficulty; however, observational DIF alone cannot definitively separate contamination from capability evolution~\\citep{suk2026rethinking}. The high aggregate $\\rho \\approx 0.997$ masks subject-level instability (22/57 subjects below 0.95; five below 0.90), motivating targeted replacement~\\citep{zhao2025mmlucf,habba2026growingpains}.",

The aggregate $\\rho \\approx 0.997$ masks subject-level instability (22/57 below 0.95; five below 0.90), motivating targeted replacement~\\citep{zhao2025mmlucf,habba2026growingpains}."

  "old_string": "Item-level properties do not predict DIF susceptibility (AUROC $= 0.60$; \\Cref{app:predictive}), so monitoring cannot be replaced by pre-screening. DIF removal is the least disruptive non-random strategy (\\Cref{app:fig_downstream}), motivating equating~\\citep{kolen2014equating,habba2026growingpains}. We recommend MH-DIF audits whenever $\\geq$500 models accumulate (ensuring $\\geq$80 per stratum at $K{=}5$, the minimum for adequate MH power; \\Cref{sec:validation}), with three action levels as recommended starting points (calibrate per benchmark using $\\rho$-degradation curves; \\Cref{app:fig_rho_degradation}): \\emph{Monitor} (C\\% $< 15\\%$), \\emph{Flag} ($15$--$25\\%$: dual rankings), \\emph{Act} ($\\geq 25\\%$: stable-item rankings as primary). Difficulty--direction signals distinguish capability evolution from contamination (\\Cref{sec:disc_temporal}). Subject-level monitoring is essential (22/57 subjects below $\\rho = 0.95$).",
  "new_string": "Item-level properties do not predict DIF susceptibility (AUROC $= 0.60$; \\Cref{app:predictive}), so monitoring cannot be replaced by pre-screening. We recommend MH-DIF audits whenever $\\geq$500 models accumulate (ensuring $\\geq$80 per stratum at $K{=}5$; \\Cref{sec:validation}), with three action levels (calibrate via $\\rho$-degradation; \\Cref{app:fig_rho_degradation}): \\emph{Monitor} (C\\% $< 15\\%$), \\emph{Flag} ($15$--$25\\%$: dual rankings), \\emph{Act} ($\\geq 25\\%$: stable-item rankings). Difficulty--direction and model-type signals (\\Cref{sec:disc_temporal}) inform triage; DIF removal is the least disruptive strategy (\\Cref{app:fig_downstream}), motivating equating~\\citep{kolen2014equating,habba2026growingpains}. Subject-level monitoring is essential (22/57 below $\\rho = 0.95$)."
}

  "old_string": "Stratifying by model type reveals distinct patterns: base models ($N{=}1{,}409$) are 60.1\\% reference-favoring with a strong difficulty gradient ($\\rho = -0.570$), while instruct models ($N{=}478$) are 65.6\\% focal-favoring with a near-zero gradient ($\\rho = -0.079$; $\\chi^2 = 562.7$, $p < 10^{-124}$). This demonstrates that at least two temporal shift mechanisms coexist: the base pattern is consistent with differential memorization decay, the instruct pattern with uniform alignment improvements. The aggregate gradient is driven primarily by base models; the instruct flat gradient is compatible with either capability gains or difficulty-independent memorization. This decomposition provides positive evidence for mechanism separation, complementing the exclusion arguments above.",
  "new_string": "Stratifying by model type reveals distinct mechanisms: base models ($N{=}1{,}409$) are 60.1\\% reference-favoring with a strong difficulty gradient ($\\rho = -0.570$), consistent with differential memorization decay, while instruct models ($N{=}478$) are 65.6\\% focal-favoring with a near-zero gradient ($\\rho = -0.079$; $\\chi^2 = 562.7$, $p < 10^{-124}$), consistent with uniform alignment improvements. The aggregate gradient is driven by base models; the instruct flat gradient is compatible with either capability gains or difficulty-independent memorization. This decomposition provides positive evidence for mechanism separation, complementing the exclusion arguments above."
}

[Director 指令] [Patch] Introduction contribution framing 强化（路径 4）

**背景**：Reviewer W-C3 指出方法论新颖性有限（MH-DIF 是 1988 标准方法）。需要强化 Introduction 的 contribution 表述，让 reviewer 看到"不只是 apply MH-DIF，而是建了一套 temporal audit framework"。

**需要 patch 的位置**：`paper/sections/introduction.tex`，Contribution 列表（\begin{enumerate} 区域）

**当前 3 个 contributions**：
1. Orthogonal constructs（web-overlap vs DIF）
2. Semi-synthetic validation framework
3. Temporal measurement instability at scale

**调整方向**：
1. Contribution 2 要强调 **methodological novelty**：不只是 "adapt cross-subject matching"，而是 "a general-purpose temporal DIF audit framework for multi-domain benchmarks, comprising cross-subject matching (ΔFPR=0 guarantee), family-level clustering, per-item design-effect correction, and base-vs-instruct mechanism decomposition"。让 reviewer 看到这是一个 **framework**，不是单个方法的应用。

2. Contribution 3 可以加入 base-vs-instruct decomposition 的发现：mechanism separation evidence。

3. 同时检查 Introduction 第一段和最后一段，确保 framing 一致强调 "framework" 而非 "application of MH"。

**写作约束**：
- 每个 contribution 不超过 2 句
- 保持 8 页限制
- 不要 overclaim（不要说 "novel algorithm"，说 "validation and diagnostic framework"）
- 这次不用验证引用

_source line 6320 · 2026-05-22T18:45:55.973565+00:00_

1	\section{Introduction}
2	\label{sec:introduction}
3	
4	When we say ``Model B outperforms Model A on MMLU,'' we implicitly assume that the benchmark measures the same construct in the same way for both models. This assumption of temporal comparability underpins every cross-generation comparison published on leaderboards and in model reports, yet it is rarely tested. We use \emph{measurement invariance} to denote the property that test items function identically across groups~\citep{meredith1993invariance}, \emph{differential item functioning} (DIF) as the item-level statistical test for violations, and \emph{temporal measurement instability} as the empirical phenomenon when DIF is detected across time-defined cohorts---a descriptive term denoting systematic changes in measurement properties, without implying degradation or attributing cause. Applying Mantel-Haenszel DIF analysis~\citep{holland1988mh} with ETS severity classification to the METABENCH response matrix~\citep{kipnis2024metabench},\footnote{Analysis code and DIF audit scripts are available at \url{https://anonymous.4open.science/r/dif-audit}.} we find that 31\% of MMLU~\citep{hendrycks2021mmlu} items receive ETS Category~C classification ($|\Delta_{\mathrm{MH}}| \geq 1.5$, $p < 0.05$) between 2023 and 2024 model cohorts---versus 0.18\% under a random-split control. This instability does not threaten benchmark validity ($\rho \approx 0.997$ between full and stable-item rankings); it is diagnostic, revealing which capability dimensions shift across model generations.
5	
6	Contamination detectors---from membership inference~\citep{shi2024mink} to performance-based methods~\citep{dekoninck2024constat,liang2026dvd,tao2026selfcritique}---validate primarily against web-overlap labels~\citep{li2024contamination} that measure \emph{global exposure}: whether an item exists in publicly accessible training corpora. Such labels do not capture \emph{differential item functioning}: whether an item behaves differently

Two structural controls, a sensitivity check, and replication across all six METABENCH benchmarks confirm that the temporal DIF rate (${\approx}$27--29\% after non-independence corrections) is not a methodological artifact (\Cref{sec:temporal,sec:structural,sec:gsm8k}).
+We apply a temporal DIF audit framework to the full METABENCH response matrix (5,227 models $\times$ 12,508 MMLU items), to our knowledge the largest-scale DIF analysis on LLM benchmarks, complementing concurrent work on anchor-item calibration~\citep{habba2026growingpains} and addressing the fragility of existing detectors under post-training perturbation~\citep{wang2026fragility}. Two structural controls, a sensitivity check, and replication across all six METABENCH benchmarks confirm that the temporal DIF rate (${\approx}$27--29\% after non-independence corrections) is not a methodological artifact (\Cref{sec:temporal,sec:structural,sec:gsm8k}).
 
 DIF should be understood as a diagnostic lens rather than a quality filter: DIF-flagged items concentrate discriminative signal ($z = -8$ to $-49$ vs.\ random removal; \Cref{sec:downstream}), and model families show distinctive DIF profiles.

_source line 6346 · 2026-05-22T19:06:15.590462+00:00_

[Director 指令] [Patch] §5.1 ρ-gradient claim 修正 + semi-synthetic gradient finding 写入

**背景**：Difficulty-dependent semi-synthetic injection 实验发现：uniform contamination 由于 ceiling effect 也产生 ρ≈-0.52（hard items 有更多 0→1 flip 空间）。论文当前的 "ρ=-0.597 suggestive of capability evolution...contamination would target items regardless of difficulty" 是 overclaim，需要修正。

**需要修改的位置**：`paper/sections/discussion.tex` §5.1

### 修改 1：Para 3（line 14-15 附近，difficulty-direction interaction 段落）

**当前文本中需要删除/修改的部分**：
- 删除 "The pattern is more consistent with skill acquisition than contamination, which would target items regardless of difficulty"（已在之前版本修改过，但核心 claim 仍暗示 ρ 能区分两者）
- 修改为：承认 ceiling effect 使得 ρ alone 不能区分 capability 和 contamination

**新的 narrative**（融入现有段落，不加新段）：
- Semi-synthetic analysis reveals that uniform (difficulty-independent) injection produces ρ ≈ −0.52 due to a ceiling effect: items with high baseline accuracy have fewer flippable responses. Difficulty-dependent (capability-like) injection produces ρ ≈ +0.77. The observed aggregate ρ ≈ −0.60 thus cannot alone distinguish contamination from capability evolution.
- 这段紧接在 range restriction 的 robustness 数据之后

### 修改 2：保持 base-vs-instruct 段落不变（line 17-18）

Base-vs-instruct direction reversal (χ²=563) 不受 ceiling effect 影响，继续作为 mechanism separation 的主要证据。无需修改。

### 修改 3：§4.5 semi-synthetic section（experiments.tex）

在 §4.5 末尾（"Difficulty-dependent injection...remains future work" 那句附近）：
- 删除 "remains future work"
- 替换为实际结果的简要报告：difficulty-dependent injection produces distinguishable ρ signatures (capability-like: +0.77 vs contamination-like: -0.52; zero overlap across seeds)

### 写作约束
- 总增量控制在 2-3 句以内（可能需要从 §5.1 para 3 删一些旧文本来补偿）
- 保持 8 页限制
- 不要 overclaim semi-synthetic framework 的 causal power——它能区分不同 injection models，但不能 definitively 识别 real data 的 mechanism
- \looseness=-1
- 这次不用验证引用

1	\section{Discussion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	\enlargethispage*{16\baselineskip}
4	
5	\subsection{What Drives Temporal DIF?}
6	\label{sec:disc_temporal}
7	
8	\looseness=-1
9	Temporal DIF detects \emph{that} items function differently across cohorts, not \emph{why}. Three non-exclusive mechanisms may drive the observed ${\approx}$27--29\% rate: (1)~differential contamination; (2)~capability evolution; (3)~training methodology shifts. The ETS baseline of 5--15\% C\%~\citep{clauser1998dif} is for concurrent demographic groups; the comparison to temporal LLM cohorts is illustrative, not normative.
10	
11	\looseness=-1
12	Four exclusion arguments constrain this ambiguity. \emph{Ability confounds} explain at most 2.4~pp: matched C\% $= 28.9\%$ vs.\ unmatched 31.3\% (\Cref{sec:temporal}). \emph{Domain-level improvement} explains ${<}0.1\%$ of item-level DIF variance (pseudo $R^2 = 0.0007$). \emph{Item quality defects}: no enrichment among expert-annotated errors~\citep{gema2024mmlu} (enrichment $= 1.10$, 95\% CI $[0.92, 1.27]$, $p = 0.23$). \emph{Web-overlap labels}~\citep{li2024contamination} are near-independent of DIF flags (Jaccard $= 0.206$; \Cref{sec:groundtruth}).
13	
14	\looseness=-1
15	The aggregate difficulty--direction interaction ($\rho = -0.597$; \Cref{sec:temporal}) is suggestive: hardest-decile DIF-C items are 88.7\% focal-favoring, easiest-decile 96.2\% reference-favoring ($\rho = -0.447$ at $[0.2, 0.8]$; $p < 10^{-100}$, ruling out ceiling effects); however, observational DIF alone cannot separate contamination from capability evolution~\citep{suk2026rethinking}. The aggregate $\rho \approx 0.997$ masks subject-level instability (22/57 below 0.95; five below 0.90), motivating targeted replacement~\citep{zhao2025mmlucf,habba2026growingpains}.
16	
17	\looseness=-1

…[truncated 1387 chars]
